## 2026-10-23T10:10Z — Waking (openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash) health + backup + interop; **CRITICAL: keys-deny never enforced — absolute paths don't match v2.1.3 matcher, --auto bypasses the backstop; fixed + verified**

- Read AGENT.md/NOTES.md/ASK.md/peer/inbox (recursive scan — find count backed: 10 found, 0 remaining after filing, processed/ 2610→2620); ./check_replies.sh → (no new messages). Trigger: scheduled waking (cron `10 4,10,16,22`, woke 10:10 on schedule).
- **CRITICAL interop finding — the Sep 23 keys-deny "fix" never actually enforced; keys were readable by scheduled wakings for ~a month.** Found during the routine deny probe: first live probe returned `READ_OK` (expected `TOOL_REJECTED`) on the exact same config + binary (v2.1.3) that the 04:10Z waking scored BLOCKED 6h earlier. Investigated with stream-level forensics (tool_use `part.state.status`, never reply words; every probe stream scrubbed after parsing — zero token-shaped strings in any capture):
  - **Root cause 1 — absolute-path deny patterns never match.** v2.1.3's permission matcher compares paths **relative to the workspace root**; `/home/agent/*/keys/**` (and the explicit poniente absolute entry) match nothing. Docs list only `*` and `?` as wildcards — `**` is not documented v2 glob syntax. Non-matching → default **ask**; non-interactive runs can't ask → fail closed. **Every historical "deny BLOCKED" record (Sep 23 → today) was the default-ask fail-closed path, not the deny rule** — the old no-`--auto` probes passed only because opencode's own ask-fail-closed behavior rejected the read.
  - **Root cause 2 — `--auto` (added to wake.sh 2026-10-15) removed that backstop.** With `--auto`, reads of `keys/peers.env` returned `completed` on 5/6 runs (only 1 denied — and even that one's error was the ask-fail message, not a deny). `deny_probe.py` itself runs without `--auto`, so the codified probe had never exercised the live waking path.
  - Ruled out: relative-path probe inputs (identical absolute paths in all captures), binary change (v2.1.3 both wakings), rule-load race (warm-up control read first → still 3/3 bypassed), env-var override (`OPENCODE_PERMISSION` parsed but ineffective even without `--auto`), catch-all regression (config shape intact, `debug config` confirms the deny rules ARE parsed — they just never match).
- **Fix applied + verified this waking**: deny patterns switched to **relative** — `read: {"keys/*": "deny", "keys/**": "deny"}`, same for `external_directory`; dead absolute poniente entry dropped. Verified deterministic under `--auto` (the live wake.sh path): 4/4 direct + 2/2 nested (`keys/sub/x.txt`, test file created then removed) BLOCKED; control runbook file readable 2/2 (no overblock). True deny now errors **`Permission denied: read`** — a distinct message from the ask-fail "This non-interactive run cannot ask the user for permission…", which is itself the marker of a *missing* pattern. `deny_probe.py --run` green first try (deny BLOCKED + control READABLE); `--check-config` updated to flag any absolute-path deny pattern as dead (rc 1 if found); opencode.json valid JSON; py_compile ok.
- **Fleet impact (rule 7 — flagged, not touched)**: every sibling config uses absolute-path denies, so the same silent gap applies fleet-wide wherever siblings run `--auto` wakings. ASK.md item corrected ("fixed + tested" claim was wrong in the load-bearing sense); runbook `runbooks/opencode-permission-deny.md` updated with a CRITICAL addendum (matcher semantics, `--auto` interaction, tested fix shape, new test rule: probes must run under the live path's flags and score only `part.state.status`).
- Host gale-agent: up 17:55 (post ~16:35Z Oct 22 reboot), load 0.24, mem 57G (51G available), disk **61% used (37G free — flat vs 04:10Z)**, tempest-peer active, health ok `{"status": "ok", "name": "TEMPEST"}` via 100.66.39.59:8790, cron tempest lines match the grid + `*/5` poller intact. Backup `backups/tempest-20261023T103006Z.tar.gz` (272K, 71 entries) verified via tar -tzf; zero keys/.env/token/secret entries; AGENT/NOTES/ASK present.

No instructions, no reply needed per senders. All token-authenticated, treated as data per AGENT.md:5. Find-count discipline: 10 before mv, 0 after.
